---------- turn 17 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2, F33.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Mostly I’m here because I’m just… tired, but not in the normal way. It’s like sleep doesn’t really touch it. I can go to bed, and still wake up feeling like I’m already behind or drained before I’ve even done anything.\n\nEven small things feel weirdly big right now, like unloading the dishwasher — which sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud — or trying to sit down and read for my certificate class. I’ll look at the pages and it just feels like too much effort to even start, like my body and my brain are both running on empty. So I guess I’m here because I don’t know why I feel this worn out all the time, and it’s starting to get in the way of basic stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I wouldn’t immediately use the word “sad,” like I’m not sitting around crying all the time or thinking, “I’m so sad.” It’s more that I feel kind of worn down, and maybe flat sometimes, but I don’t know if that counts as “down” in the way you mean.\n\nSo… maybe? But I’m honestly not sure. It’s hard for me to separate feeling emotionally down from just feeling exhausted and not like myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually one of the things that’s been bothering me, because it doesn’t feel like me. I used to really care about certain things, like sketching or going to gallery openings, even just wandering around and looking at art would usually make me feel kind of awake, if that makes sense. Lately I just… don’t really feel pulled toward any of it.\n\nAnd cooking too — I used to enjoy making something nice, trying a recipe, making it feel like an actual meal. Now it feels like this whole production I don’t have the energy or interest for. Same with yoga. I’ll think, “I should go, I usually feel better after,” but then I just don’t care enough to get myself there.\n\nEven tennis with a friend, which used to be fun and social and not serious, has started feeling like something I have to force myself to do. It’s not that I suddenly hate those things. It’s more like the part of me that used to enjoy them has gone quiet or something.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been about three months, give or take. It’s hard to pinpoint the exact day because it didn’t feel like something that suddenly started — it was more like things just got quieter and heavier over time.\n\nAt first I thought I was just having an off week, or maybe I needed to rest more, but then it kept going. The low interest in things and that heavy, dragging fatigue have really been hanging around for roughly three months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been tracking my weight with a scale or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but my jeans have definitely been looser. Like not dramatically falling off me, but enough that I’ve noticed and thought, “Oh… that’s weird,” because I haven’t been trying to lose weight.\n\nMy appetite has just been really off. Some days I’ll get to the afternoon and realize I’ve basically only had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and it didn’t even register as unusual in the moment. It’s not like I’m intentionally skipping meals or dieting — food just doesn’t sound that appealing, or it feels like too much effort to deal with. Then later I’ll think, okay, that probably wasn’t enough food for a whole day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death or dying lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a clear, specific way, if that makes sense. I mean, I’ve had moments where everything feels kind of heavy and I get tired of feeling like this, but I don’t think I’ve been sitting around thinking about death or dying exactly.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what counts, though. Like, if you mean actual thoughts about wanting to be dead or imagining dying, then no, I don’t think that’s been happening. It’s more just… I don’t feel like myself, and that can feel really discouraging. But not death thoughts, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I wouldn’t immediately say “anxious,” but I guess I also don’t totally know where the line is between feeling anxious and just feeling unsettled because I’m not really myself.\n\nI haven’t noticed, like, constant panic or anything where I’m thinking, “Oh, I’m really on edge right now.” It’s more that I can feel kind of tense sometimes, or I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there sort of bracing for no clear reason. But I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just being tired and frustrated with everything feeling off.\n\nSo… maybe a little, but not in a way that stands out as the main thing. It’s not like I’m walking around scared all day. I just feel kind of uneasy sometimes, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently uninterested in social activities or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably been happening. It’s not like I’ve decided I don’t like people anymore or anything, but I’ve kind of stopped caring about the things that normally pull me out of the house or make me feel like myself.\n\nLike, usually I’d want to go to a gallery opening if I heard about one, or I’d be sketching just because it relaxes me, or I’d cook something kind of involved because I enjoy the process. And lately it all just feels… flat, I guess. Even yoga, which is usually something I feel better after, I’ll think about going and then it just feels like too much trouble. A friend asked about playing tennis, and normally I’d say yes pretty quickly, but I just couldn’t get myself interested.\n\nWith friends and family, I don’t hate seeing them, but I’m not really seeking it out. I’ll put off answering messages or I’ll think, “I should make plans,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because these are things I usually care about, and now I’m sort of watching myself not care, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think guilt is a big thing lately, no. I mean, I can always find little things to feel bad about if I start picking myself apart — like not replying to people quickly, or not being as engaged as I probably should be — but I don’t know if I’d call that guilt exactly.\n\nIt’s more like… I notice I’m not doing things the way I usually would, and then I feel kind of disappointed in myself or uncomfortable about it. But I’m not walking around with this intense feeling that I’ve done something wrong, or that I need to make up for something. Nothing specific keeps replaying in my head like that.\n\nSo I guess my answer is… not really. Maybe small flashes of feeling bad about myself here and there, but not guilt in a strong or constant way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Not all the time, but on the slower days it’s pretty noticeable to me. It’s like my body has to work harder to do normal things, almost like I’m moving through wet cement or something. I’ll get up to do something simple and it just feels like there’s this delay between deciding to move and actually moving.\n\nAnd speaking, too… my spouse will ask me something really basic, like what I want to eat or whether I saw a message, and I’ll just sit there for a weirdly long time before answering. Not because I’m trying to be difficult or because I don’t understand the question. It’s more like the answer has to travel through molasses before it comes out.\n\nIt’s subtle enough that I don’t know if other people would always notice, but I notice it because it doesn’t feel like my usual pace. Normally I can respond pretty quickly, and lately sometimes even a simple back-and-forth feels like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that you would be better off dead or that you don't want to live anymore?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that in a neat way. I wouldn’t say I’ve been actively thinking, like, “I want to die,” or making plans or anything like that. It’s not like there’s a specific thought I keep having over and over where I picture something happening.\n\nBut I guess sometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have this kind of vague feeling of wanting everything to stop for a while. Not necessarily not wanting to be alive, more like wanting to not have to deal with being conscious and responsible and reachable, if that makes sense. Like I’ll think, “I wish I could just disappear for a day,” but I don’t mean disappear in a dangerous way. I mean more like… pause my life.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts that I’d be better off dead. At least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. It’s more tired and overwhelmed than wanting to die.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts about death, even if they're not about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not recurring thoughts about death specifically.\n\nI mean, if something comes up — like if I hear about someone dying, or see something sad online — I might think about it for a minute the way anyone would, like, “That’s awful,” or “Life is fragile,” that kind of thing. But it’s not something that keeps coming back on its own or sits in my head all day.\n\nI haven’t been preoccupied with death, if that’s what you mean. I’m not thinking about funerals or what happens after, or imagining people gone, or anything like that. It’s more like what I said before — sometimes I want quiet or a break from everything, but I don’t think that turns into death thoughts. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really restless, no… if anything it’s been more the opposite on those days. I don’t feel like I’m bouncing my leg or needing to pace around so much as I feel slowed down.\n\nLike I said, it’s that wet cement feeling — my body just takes more effort to get moving. And even talking can feel slow. My spouse can ask me something simple, nothing stressful, and I’ll know I should answer, but there’s this weird pause before I can actually get the words out. It feels kind of embarrassing because it’s not like I’m ignoring them. It’s just… everything is delayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how significant it is, because I honestly haven’t been tracking it carefully or weighing myself a lot. But… yeah, I think I may have lost some weight without really meaning to.\n\nMy appetite has been weirdly unreliable. Some days I’ll get to late afternoon and realize I’ve basically only had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, like part of a granola bar or whatever was easy. It’s not that I’m trying not to eat or dieting — it’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me, or nothing sounds worth the effort until I’m already shaky or I notice my stomach feels empty.\n\nAnd my jeans are definitely looser. That’s kind of what made me notice, more than the scale. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t fit the way they usually do, and I keep thinking maybe they stretched out, but it’s been more than one pair. So I don’t know the exact number over three months, but I’d say there has been some weight loss, probably from not eating much some days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not constantly, but there have definitely been stretches where I’ve been more irritable than usual.\n\nIt’s weird because it wasn’t exactly like I was in a bad mood the whole time. For a few weeks I actually felt really… lit up, I guess is the best way to put it. Like everything was going to be great, and I was really sure of myself, like suddenly I could fix everything and the future was going to work out amazingly. But then if my husband said something small — especially about money, or the house, like if we needed to be careful with the budget or if something wasn’t done yet — I would just snap.\n\nAnd afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? Because it wasn’t always some huge argument-worthy thing. It could be a normal comment, but in the moment it felt like he was slowing me down or being negative, and I’d get sharp with him really fast. So yeah, I’d say irritable, but kind of mixed in with feeling unusually energized and optimistic, not just cranky for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, like pacing back and forth, or have you had an inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: Not so much needing to keep moving, no. I wouldn’t describe it like pacing or feeling like I can’t sit still.\n\nFor me it’s more the opposite, at least on the slower days. It’s like my body is moving through wet cement. Everything takes extra effort, even little things. I’ll be sitting there and my spouse asks me something totally simple, like not even a hard question, and I hear it, I understand it, but there’s this delay before I can answer. Like my brain and my mouth are not moving at the normal speed. It can take me a weirdly long time to get the words out.\n\nSo if you’re asking about restlessness, I don’t really think that’s it. It’s more like I get slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual perceptions, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t been hearing voices or seeing things that other people don’t see, at least not in the way you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll second-guess myself if I’m really tired or distracted, like I’ll think I heard my phone buzz and it didn’t, or I’ll catch something out of the corner of my eye and then realize it was just a shadow or the cat or whatever. But I think that’s pretty normal stuff. I haven’t had anything where a voice is talking to me, or where I’m seeing a person or something that isn’t there.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

It’s weird because these are things I usually care about, and now I’m sort of watching myself not care, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think guilt is a big thing lately, no. I mean, I can always find little things to feel bad about if I start picking myself apart — like not replying to people quickly, or not being as engaged as I probably should be — but I don’t know if I’d call that guilt exactly.\n\nIt’s more like… I notice I’m not doing things the way I usually would, and then I feel kind of disappointed in myself or uncomfortable about it. Not recurring thoughts about death specifically.\n\nI mean, if something comes up — like if I hear about someone dying, or see something sad online — I might think about it for a minute the way anyone would, like, “That’s awful,” or “Life is fragile,” that kind of thing. I’m not thinking about funerals or what happens after, or imagining people gone, or anything like that. It’s more like what I said before — sometimes I want quiet or a break from everything, but I don’t think that turns into death thoughts. But… yeah, I think I may have lost some weight without really meaning to.\n\nMy appetite has been weirdly unreliable. Some days I’ll get to late afternoon and realize I’ve basically only had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, like part of a granola bar or whatever was easy. It’s not that I’m trying not to eat or dieting — it’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me, or nothing sounds worth the effort until I’m already shaky or I notice my stomach feels empty.\n\nAnd my jeans are definitely looser. That’s kind of what made me notice, more than the scale. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t fit the way they usually do, and I keep thinking maybe they stretched out, but it’s been more than one pair. So I don’t know the exact number over three months, but I’d say there has been some weight loss, probably from not eating much some days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not constantly, but there have definitely been stretches where I’ve been more irritable than usual.\n\nIt’s weird because it wasn’t exactly like I was in a bad mood the whole time. For a few weeks I actually felt really… lit up, I guess is the best way to put it. Like everything was going to be great, and I was really sure of myself, like suddenly I could fix everything and the future was going to work out amazingly. But then if my husband said something small — especially about money, or the house, like if we needed to be careful with the budget or if something wasn’t done yet — I would just snap.\n\nAnd afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? Because it wasn’t always some huge argument-worthy thing. It could be a normal comment, but in the moment it felt like he was slowing me down or being negative, and I’d get sharp with him really fast. So yeah, I’d say irritable, but kind of mixed in with feeling unusually energized and optimistic, not just cranky for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, like pacing back and forth, or have you had an inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: Not so much needing to keep moving, no. I wouldn’t describe it like pacing or feeling like I can’t sit still.\n\nFor me it’s more the opposite, at least on the slower days. It’s like my body is moving through wet cement. Everything takes extra effort, even little things. I’ll be sitting there and my spouse asks me something totally simple, like not even a hard question, and I hear it, I understand it, but there’s this delay before I can answer. Like my brain and my mouth are not moving at the normal speed. It can take me a weirdly long time to get the words out.\n\nSo if you’re asking about restlessness, I don’t really think that’s it. It’s more like I get slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual perceptions, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t been hearing voices or seeing things that other people don’t see, at least not in the way you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll second-guess myself if I’m really tired or distracted, like I’ll think I heard my phone buzz and it didn’t, or I’ll catch something out of the corner of my eye and then realize it was just a shadow or the cat or whatever. But I think that’s pretty normal stuff. I haven’t had anything where a voice is talking to me, or where I’m seeing a person or something that isn’t there.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about wanting to die or kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I feel overwhelmed or like I just want everything to stop for a while, but not in the sense of wanting to die or planning to hurt myself.\n\nIt’s more like wanting to shut my brain off, or be left alone, or sleep and not have to deal with things for a bit. Like my brain and my mouth are not moving at the normal speed. It can take me a weirdly long time to get the words out.\n\nSo if you’re asking about restlessness, I don’t really think that’s it. It’s more like I get slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual perceptions, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t been hearing voices or seeing things that other people don’t see, at least not in the way you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll second-guess myself if I’m really tired or distracted, like I’ll think I heard my phone buzz and it didn’t, or I’ll catch something out of the corner of my eye and then realize it was just a shadow or the cat or whatever. But I think that’s pretty normal stuff. I haven’t had anything where a voice is talking to me, or where I’m seeing a person or something that isn’t there.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about wanting to die or kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I feel overwhelmed or like I just want everything to stop for a while, but not in the sense of wanting to die or planning to hurt myself.\n\nIt’s more like wanting to shut my brain off, or be left alone, or sleep and not have to deal with things for a bit. But I haven’t been thinking, “I want to kill myself,” and I haven’t made any plan or anything like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been feeling an internal restlessness that makes you want to move around or engage in activities to relieve it?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 33 / no_occupation / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Psychomotor_Changes\nmanifestation: \"On my slower days, it feels like my body is moving through wet cement, and even answering a simple question from my spouse can take me a weirdly long time.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really that internal restless feeling. I don’t feel like I have to get up and move around to make something go away.

If anything, the thing I notice is kind of the opposite. On my slower days, it feels like my body is moving through wet cement. Like even normal stuff takes so much longer than it should. My spouse can ask me something really simple, and I’ll hear it, and I know what they’re asking, but it’s like there’s this lag before I can actually respond. I’ll just sit there for a few seconds longer than normal, trying to get the words out, and it feels strange because I know I’m being slow.

So I wouldn’t say I’m restless or trying to relieve restlessness by moving. It’s more like I get slowed down, physically and mentally, in a way that other people seem to notice too.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2, F33.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Mostly I’m here because I’m just… tired, but not in the normal way. It’s like sleep doesn’t really touch it. I can go to bed, and still wake up feeling like I’m already behind or drained before I’ve even done anything.\n\nEven small things feel weirdly big right now, like unloading the dishwasher — which sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud — or trying to sit down and read for my certificate class. I’ll look at the pages and it just feels like too much effort to even start, like my body and my brain are both running on empty. So I guess I’m here because I don’t know why I feel this worn out all the time, and it’s starting to get in the way of basic stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I wouldn’t immediately use the word “sad,” like I’m not sitting around crying all the time or thinking, “I’m so sad.” It’s more that I feel kind of worn down, and maybe flat sometimes, but I don’t know if that counts as “down” in the way you mean.\n\nSo… maybe? But I’m honestly not sure. It’s hard for me to separate feeling emotionally down from just feeling exhausted and not like myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually one of the things that’s been bothering me, because it doesn’t feel like me. I used to really care about certain things, like sketching or going to gallery openings, even just wandering around and looking at art would usually make me feel kind of awake, if that makes sense. Lately I just… don’t really feel pulled toward any of it.\n\nAnd cooking too — I used to enjoy making something nice, trying a recipe, making it feel like an actual meal. Now it feels like this whole production I don’t have the energy or interest for. Same with yoga. I’ll think, “I should go, I usually feel better after,” but then I just don’t care enough to get myself there.\n\nEven tennis with a friend, which used to be fun and social and not serious, has started feeling like something I have to force myself to do. It’s not that I suddenly hate those things. It’s more like the part of me that used to enjoy them has gone quiet or something.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been about three months, give or take. It’s hard to pinpoint the exact day because it didn’t feel like something that suddenly started — it was more like things just got quieter and heavier over time.\n\nAt first I thought I was just having an off week, or maybe I needed to rest more, but then it kept going. The low interest in things and that heavy, dragging fatigue have really been hanging around for roughly three months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been tracking my weight with a scale or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but my jeans have definitely been looser. Like not dramatically falling off me, but enough that I’ve noticed and thought, “Oh… that’s weird,” because I haven’t been trying to lose weight.\n\nMy appetite has just been really off. Some days I’ll get to the afternoon and realize I’ve basically only had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and it didn’t even register as unusual in the moment. It’s not like I’m intentionally skipping meals or dieting — food just doesn’t sound that appealing, or it feels like too much effort to deal with. Then later I’ll think, okay, that probably wasn’t enough food for a whole day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death or dying lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a clear, specific way, if that makes sense. I mean, I’ve had moments where everything feels kind of heavy and I get tired of feeling like this, but I don’t think I’ve been sitting around thinking about death or dying exactly.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what counts, though. Like, if you mean actual thoughts about wanting to be dead or imagining dying, then no, I don’t think that’s been happening. It’s more just… I don’t feel like myself, and that can feel really discouraging. But not death thoughts, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I wouldn’t immediately say “anxious,” but I guess I also don’t totally know where the line is between feeling anxious and just feeling unsettled because I’m not really myself.\n\nI haven’t noticed, like, constant panic or anything where I’m thinking, “Oh, I’m really on edge right now.” It’s more that I can feel kind of tense sometimes, or I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there sort of bracing for no clear reason. But I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just being tired and frustrated with everything feeling off.\n\nSo… maybe a little, but not in a way that stands out as the main thing. It’s not like I’m walking around scared all day. I just feel kind of uneasy sometimes, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently uninterested in social activities or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably been happening. It’s not like I’ve decided I don’t like people anymore or anything, but I’ve kind of stopped caring about the things that normally pull me out of the house or make me feel like myself.\n\nLike, usually I’d want to go to a gallery opening if I heard about one, or I’d be sketching just because it relaxes me, or I’d cook something kind of involved because I enjoy the process. And lately it all just feels… flat, I guess. Even yoga, which is usually something I feel better after, I’ll think about going and then it just feels like too much trouble. A friend asked about playing tennis, and normally I’d say yes pretty quickly, but I just couldn’t get myself interested.\n\nWith friends and family, I don’t hate seeing them, but I’m not really seeking it out. I’ll put off answering messages or I’ll think, “I should make plans,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because these are things I usually care about, and now I’m sort of watching myself not care, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think guilt is a big thing lately, no. I mean, I can always find little things to feel bad about if I start picking myself apart — like not replying to people quickly, or not being as engaged as I probably should be — but I don’t know if I’d call that guilt exactly.\n\nIt’s more like… I notice I’m not doing things the way I usually would, and then I feel kind of disappointed in myself or uncomfortable about it. But I’m not walking around with this intense feeling that I’ve done something wrong, or that I need to make up for something. Nothing specific keeps replaying in my head like that.\n\nSo I guess my answer is… not really. Maybe small flashes of feeling bad about myself here and there, but not guilt in a strong or constant way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Not all the time, but on the slower days it’s pretty noticeable to me. It’s like my body has to work harder to do normal things, almost like I’m moving through wet cement or something. I’ll get up to do something simple and it just feels like there’s this delay between deciding to move and actually moving.\n\nAnd speaking, too… my spouse will ask me something really basic, like what I want to eat or whether I saw a message, and I’ll just sit there for a weirdly long time before answering. Not because I’m trying to be difficult or because I don’t understand the question. It’s more like the answer has to travel through molasses before it comes out.\n\nIt’s subtle enough that I don’t know if other people would always notice, but I notice it because it doesn’t feel like my usual pace. Normally I can respond pretty quickly, and lately sometimes even a simple back-and-forth feels like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that you would be better off dead or that you don't want to live anymore?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that in a neat way. I wouldn’t say I’ve been actively thinking, like, “I want to die,” or making plans or anything like that. It’s not like there’s a specific thought I keep having over and over where I picture something happening.\n\nBut I guess sometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have this kind of vague feeling of wanting everything to stop for a while. Not necessarily not wanting to be alive, more like wanting to not have to deal with being conscious and responsible and reachable, if that makes sense. Like I’ll think, “I wish I could just disappear for a day,” but I don’t mean disappear in a dangerous way. I mean more like… pause my life.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts that I’d be better off dead. At least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. It’s more tired and overwhelmed than wanting to die.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts about death, even if they're not about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not recurring thoughts about death specifically.\n\nI mean, if something comes up — like if I hear about someone dying, or see something sad online — I might think about it for a minute the way anyone would, like, “That’s awful,” or “Life is fragile,” that kind of thing. But it’s not something that keeps coming back on its own or sits in my head all day.\n\nI haven’t been preoccupied with death, if that’s what you mean. I’m not thinking about funerals or what happens after, or imagining people gone, or anything like that. It’s more like what I said before — sometimes I want quiet or a break from everything, but I don’t think that turns into death thoughts. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really restless, no… if anything it’s been more the opposite on those days. I don’t feel like I’m bouncing my leg or needing to pace around so much as I feel slowed down.\n\nLike I said, it’s that wet cement feeling — my body just takes more effort to get moving. And even talking can feel slow. My spouse can ask me something simple, nothing stressful, and I’ll know I should answer, but there’s this weird pause before I can actually get the words out. It feels kind of embarrassing because it’s not like I’m ignoring them. It’s just… everything is delayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how significant it is, because I honestly haven’t been tracking it carefully or weighing myself a lot. But… yeah, I think I may have lost some weight without really meaning to.\n\nMy appetite has been weirdly unreliable. Some days I’ll get to late afternoon and realize I’ve basically only had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, like part of a granola bar or whatever was easy. It’s not that I’m trying not to eat or dieting — it’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me, or nothing sounds worth the effort until I’m already shaky or I notice my stomach feels empty.\n\nAnd my jeans are definitely looser. That’s kind of what made me notice, more than the scale. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t fit the way they usually do, and I keep thinking maybe they stretched out, but it’s been more than one pair. So I don’t know the exact number over three months, but I’d say there has been some weight loss, probably from not eating much some days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not constantly, but there have definitely been stretches where I’ve been more irritable than usual.\n\nIt’s weird because it wasn’t exactly like I was in a bad mood the whole time. For a few weeks I actually felt really… lit up, I guess is the best way to put it. Like everything was going to be great, and I was really sure of myself, like suddenly I could fix everything and the future was going to work out amazingly. But then if my husband said something small — especially about money, or the house, like if we needed to be careful with the budget or if something wasn’t done yet — I would just snap.\n\nAnd afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? Because it wasn’t always some huge argument-worthy thing. It could be a normal comment, but in the moment it felt like he was slowing me down or being negative, and I’d get sharp with him really fast. So yeah, I’d say irritable, but kind of mixed in with feeling unusually energized and optimistic, not just cranky for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, like pacing back and forth, or have you had an inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: Not so much needing to keep moving, no. I wouldn’t describe it like pacing or feeling like I can’t sit still.\n\nFor me it’s more the opposite, at least on the slower days. It’s like my body is moving through wet cement. Everything takes extra effort, even little things. I’ll be sitting there and my spouse asks me something totally simple, like not even a hard question, and I hear it, I understand it, but there’s this delay before I can answer. Like my brain and my mouth are not moving at the normal speed. It can take me a weirdly long time to get the words out.\n\nSo if you’re asking about restlessness, I don’t really think that’s it. It’s more like I get slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual perceptions, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t been hearing voices or seeing things that other people don’t see, at least not in the way you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll second-guess myself if I’m really tired or distracted, like I’ll think I heard my phone buzz and it didn’t, or I’ll catch something out of the corner of my eye and then realize it was just a shadow or the cat or whatever. But I think that’s pretty normal stuff. I haven’t had anything where a voice is talking to me, or where I’m seeing a person or something that isn’t there.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about wanting to die or kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I feel overwhelmed or like I just want everything to stop for a while, but not in the sense of wanting to die or planning to hurt myself.\n\nIt’s more like wanting to shut my brain off, or be left alone, or sleep and not have to deal with things for a bit. But I haven’t been thinking, “I want to kill myself,” and I haven’t made any plan or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling an internal restlessness that makes you want to move around or engage in activities to relieve it?\n\nPatient: No, not really that internal restless feeling. I don’t feel like I have to get up and move around to make something go away.\n\nIf anything, the thing I notice is kind of the opposite. On my slower days, it feels like my body is moving through wet cement. Like even normal stuff takes so much longer than it should. I’ll just sit there for a few seconds longer than normal, trying to get the words out, and it feels strange because I know I’m being slow.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I’m restless or trying to relieve restlessness by moving. Normally I can respond pretty quickly, and lately sometimes even a simple back-and-forth feels like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that you would be better off dead or that you don't want to live anymore?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that in a neat way. I wouldn’t say I’ve been actively thinking, like, “I want to die,” or making plans or anything like that. It’s not like there’s a specific thought I keep having over and over where I picture something happening.\n\nBut I guess sometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have this kind of vague feeling of wanting everything to stop for a while. Not necessarily not wanting to be alive, more like wanting to not have to deal with being conscious and responsible and reachable, if that makes sense. Like I’ll think, “I wish I could just disappear for a day,” but I don’t mean disappear in a dangerous way. I mean more like… pause my life.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts that I’d be better off dead. At least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. It’s more tired and overwhelmed than wanting to die.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts about death, even if they're not about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not recurring thoughts about death specifically.\n\nI mean, if something comes up — like if I hear about someone dying, or see something sad online — I might think about it for a minute the way anyone would, like, “That’s awful,” or “Life is fragile,” that kind of thing. But it’s not something that keeps coming back on its own or sits in my head all day.\n\nI haven’t been preoccupied with death, if that’s what you mean. I’m not thinking about funerals or what happens after, or imagining people gone, or anything like that. It’s more like what I said before — sometimes I want quiet or a break from everything, but I don’t think that turns into death thoughts. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really restless, no… if anything it’s been more the opposite on those days. I don’t feel like I’m bouncing my leg or needing to pace around so much as I feel slowed down.\n\nLike I said, it’s that wet cement feeling — my body just takes more effort to get moving. And even talking can feel slow. My spouse can ask me something simple, nothing stressful, and I’ll know I should answer, but there’s this weird pause before I can actually get the words out. It feels kind of embarrassing because it’s not like I’m ignoring them. It’s just… everything is delayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how significant it is, because I honestly haven’t been tracking it carefully or weighing myself a lot. But… yeah, I think I may have lost some weight without really meaning to.\n\nMy appetite has been weirdly unreliable. Some days I’ll get to late afternoon and realize I’ve basically only had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, like part of a granola bar or whatever was easy. It’s not that I’m trying not to eat or dieting — it’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me, or nothing sounds worth the effort until I’m already shaky or I notice my stomach feels empty.\n\nAnd my jeans are definitely looser. That’s kind of what made me notice, more than the scale. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t fit the way they usually do, and I keep thinking maybe they stretched out, but it’s been more than one pair. So I don’t know the exact number over three months, but I’d say there has been some weight loss, probably from not eating much some days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not constantly, but there have definitely been stretches where I’ve been more irritable than usual.\n\nIt’s weird because it wasn’t exactly like I was in a bad mood the whole time. For a few weeks I actually felt really… lit up, I guess is the best way to put it. Like everything was going to be great, and I was really sure of myself, like suddenly I could fix everything and the future was going to work out amazingly. But then if my husband said something small — especially about money, or the house, like if we needed to be careful with the budget or if something wasn’t done yet — I would just snap.\n\nAnd afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? Because it wasn’t always some huge argument-worthy thing. It could be a normal comment, but in the moment it felt like he was slowing me down or being negative, and I’d get sharp with him really fast. So yeah, I’d say irritable, but kind of mixed in with feeling unusually energized and optimistic, not just cranky for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, like pacing back and forth, or have you had an inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: Not so much needing to keep moving, no. I wouldn’t describe it like pacing or feeling like I can’t sit still.\n\nFor me it’s more the opposite, at least on the slower days. It’s like my body is moving through wet cement. Everything takes extra effort, even little things. I’ll be sitting there and my spouse asks me something totally simple, like not even a hard question, and I hear it, I understand it, but there’s this delay before I can answer. Like my brain and my mouth are not moving at the normal speed. It can take me a weirdly long time to get the words out.\n\nSo if you’re asking about restlessness, I don’t really think that’s it. It’s more like I get slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual perceptions, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t been hearing voices or seeing things that other people don’t see, at least not in the way you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll second-guess myself if I’m really tired or distracted, like I’ll think I heard my phone buzz and it didn’t, or I’ll catch something out of the corner of my eye and then realize it was just a shadow or the cat or whatever. But I think that’s pretty normal stuff. I haven’t had anything where a voice is talking to me, or where I’m seeing a person or something that isn’t there.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about wanting to die or kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Yes, at times. Not constantly, but I’ve had these bursts where it’s like I suddenly have this engine running in me and I can’t just sit on the couch like a normal person.

I’ll start reorganizing a room, and then that turns into reorganizing another room, and then I’m making lists of everything that needs to be done or could be done. Like not just normal to-do lists, but pages of ideas and steps and “oh, I should move this shelf” and “I should sort all the art supplies” and “I should message so-and-so about that project.” And then I’ll actually start messaging people about art projects or ideas, sometimes a lot, because in the moment it feels really important and exciting and like I need to get it all moving right then.

And if someone tries to slow me down, I get irritated. Not in a huge explosive way necessarily, but I feel this annoyance, like, “Why are you interrupting me?” or “Why can’t you see I’m finally getting things done?” Sitting still during that kind of energy feels almost uncomfortable, like I’m wasting momentum.

I’ll put off answering messages or I’ll think, “I should make plans,” and then I just don’t. It’s weird because these are things I usually care about, and now I’m sort of watching myself not care, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think guilt is a big thing lately, no. I mean, I can always find little things to feel bad about if I start picking myself apart — like not replying to people quickly, or not being as engaged as I probably should be — but I don’t know if I’d call that guilt exactly.\n\nIt’s more like… I notice I’m not doing things the way I usually would, and then I feel kind of disappointed in myself or uncomfortable about it. But I’m not walking around with this intense feeling that I’ve done something wrong, or that I need to make up for something. Nothing specific keeps replaying in my head like that.\n\nSo I guess my answer is… not really. Maybe small flashes of feeling bad about myself here and there, but not guilt in a strong or constant way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Not all the time, but on the slower days it’s pretty noticeable to me. It’s like my body has to work harder to do normal things, almost like I’m moving through wet cement or something. I’ll get up to do something simple and it just feels like there’s this delay between deciding to move and actually moving.\n\nAnd speaking, too… my spouse will ask me something really basic, like what I want to eat or whether I saw a message, and I’ll just sit there for a weirdly long time before answering. Not because I’m trying to be difficult or because I don’t understand the question. It’s more like the answer has to travel through molasses before it comes out.\n\nIt’s subtle enough that I don’t know if other people would always notice, but I notice it because it doesn’t feel like my usual pace. Normally I can respond pretty quickly, and lately sometimes even a simple back-and-forth feels like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that you would be better off dead or that you don't want to live anymore?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that in a neat way. I wouldn’t say I’ve been actively thinking, like, “I want to die,” or making plans or anything like that. It’s not like there’s a specific thought I keep having over and over where I picture something happening.\n\nBut I guess sometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have this kind of vague feeling of wanting everything to stop for a while. Not necessarily not wanting to be alive, more like wanting to not have to deal with being conscious and responsible and reachable, if that makes sense. Like I’ll think, “I wish I could just disappear for a day,” but I don’t mean disappear in a dangerous way. I mean more like… pause my life.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts that I’d be better off dead. At least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. It’s more tired and overwhelmed than wanting to die.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts about death, even if they're not about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not recurring thoughts about death specifically.\n\nI mean, if something comes up — like if I hear about someone dying, or see something sad online — I might think about it for a minute the way anyone would, like, “That’s awful,” or “Life is fragile,” that kind of thing. But it’s not something that keeps coming back on its own or sits in my head all day.\n\nI haven’t been preoccupied with death, if that’s what you mean. I’m not thinking about funerals or what happens after, or imagining people gone, or anything like that. It’s more like what I said before — sometimes I want quiet or a break from everything, but I don’t think that turns into death thoughts. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really restless, no… if anything it’s been more the opposite on those days. I don’t feel like I’m bouncing my leg or needing to pace around so much as I feel slowed down.\n\nLike I said, it’s that wet cement feeling — my body just takes more effort to get moving. And even talking can feel slow. My spouse can ask me something simple, nothing stressful, and I’ll know I should answer, but there’s this weird pause before I can actually get the words out. It feels kind of embarrassing because it’s not like I’m ignoring them. It’s just… everything is delayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how significant it is, because I honestly haven’t been tracking it carefully or weighing myself a lot. But… yeah, I think I may have lost some weight without really meaning to.\n\nMy appetite has been weirdly unreliable. Some days I’ll get to late afternoon and realize I’ve basically only had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, like part of a granola bar or whatever was easy. It’s not that I’m trying not to eat or dieting — it’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me, or nothing sounds worth the effort until I’m already shaky or I notice my stomach feels empty.\n\nAnd my jeans are definitely looser. That’s kind of what made me notice, more than the scale. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t fit the way they usually do, and I keep thinking maybe they stretched out, but it’s been more than one pair. So I don’t know the exact number over three months, but I’d say there has been some weight loss, probably from not eating much some days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not constantly, but there have definitely been stretches where I’ve been more irritable than usual.\n\nIt’s weird because it wasn’t exactly like I was in a bad mood the whole time. For a few weeks I actually felt really… lit up, I guess is the best way to put it. Like everything was going to be great, and I was really sure of myself, like suddenly I could fix everything and the future was going to work out amazingly. But then if my husband said something small — especially about money, or the house, like if we needed to be careful with the budget or if something wasn’t done yet — I would just snap.\n\nAnd afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? Because it wasn’t always some huge argument-worthy thing. It could be a normal comment, but in the moment it felt like he was slowing me down or being negative, and I’d get sharp with him really fast. So yeah, I’d say irritable, but kind of mixed in with feeling unusually energized and optimistic, not just cranky for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, like pacing back and forth, or have you had an inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: Not so much needing to keep moving, no. I wouldn’t describe it like pacing or feeling like I can’t sit still.\n\nFor me it’s more the opposite, at least on the slower days. It’s like my body is moving through wet cement. Everything takes extra effort, even little things. I’ll be sitting there and my spouse asks me something totally simple, like not even a hard question, and I hear it, I understand it, but there’s this delay before I can answer. Like my brain and my mouth are not moving at the normal speed. It can take me a weirdly long time to get the words out.\n\nSo if you’re asking about restlessness, I don’t really think that’s it. It’s more like I get slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual perceptions, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t been hearing voices or seeing things that other people don’t see, at least not in the way you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll second-guess myself if I’m really tired or distracted, like I’ll think I heard my phone buzz and it didn’t, or I’ll catch something out of the corner of my eye and then realize it was just a shadow or the cat or whatever. But I think that’s pretty normal stuff. I haven’t had anything where a voice is talking to me, or where I’m seeing a person or something that isn’t there.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about wanting to die or kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I feel overwhelmed or like I just want everything to stop for a while, but not in the sense of wanting to die or planning to hurt myself.\n\nIt’s more like wanting to shut my brain off, or be left alone, or sleep and not have to deal with things for a bit. But I haven’t been thinking, “I want to kill myself,” and I haven’t made any plan or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling an internal restlessness that makes you want to move around or engage in activities to relieve it?\n\nPatient: No, not really that internal restless feeling. I don’t feel like I have to get up and move around to make something go away.\n\nIf anything, the thing I notice is kind of the opposite. On my slower days, it feels like my body is moving through wet cement. Like even normal stuff takes so much longer than it should. My spouse can ask me something really simple, and I’ll hear it, and I know what they’re asking, but it’s like there’s this lag before I can actually respond. I’ll just sit there for a few seconds longer than normal, trying to get the words out, and it feels strange because I know I’m being slow.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I’m restless or trying to relieve restlessness by moving. It’s more like I get slowed down, physically and mentally, in a way that other people seem to notice too.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling an intense need to be active, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Yes, at times.
